# NEXUM — FULL OSIRIS GAP AUDIT (2026-10-07)

**Baseline audited:**
- NEXUM `e2b17d0d9ef3a4c6cda780b567abdd5b7787b48e` (production build `202610071322-z68u`).
- OSIRIS-REFERENCE commit `7a3daec48708e5d9da8e053f08812aa0f617031e` (2026-09-28, local copy, MIT): 288 TypeScript files, ~55,300 lines, 44 server API groups, `intel/server.js`, `public/`, `tools/`, docs, SDK.

**Method:**
- Six disjoint read-only audits (1 shell, map, search, PWA, SDK; 2 thematic layers, space, aviation, environment; 3 alerts, news, intelligence, AI, markets, chain; 4 routing, navigation, drawing, AOI, watch, maritime, infrastructure, ArcGIS discovery and import; 5 OSINT, cyber, intel server, backend plumbing; 6 CCTV and webcams, all 70 files under `api/cctv/`).
- Every row cites OSIRIS `file:line` and the NEXUM equivalent, read in the code of both projects.
- Licence and terms pages were fetched with NEXUM's neutral User-Agent (copies in the audit's working folders).
- Each part lists every file it read in its own introduction.

**Columns of every row:**
1. ID
2. OSIRIS control or capability
3. Component and source file
4. What it really does
5. Data, API or service used
6. Licence, terms and cost (code rights and data/API/tile/image/video/stream/service rights audited separately)
7. NEXUM current equivalent
8. Real functional gap
9. Classification
10. Final (D1 2026-10-07; D2, D4) — replaces "Under NEXUM Option A"
11. Implementation plan
12. Test and acceptance criterion

**Classification rules (as updated by the author's decisions of 2026-10-07):**
- **REUSE / ADAPT** — OSIRIS code is legally reusable: its licence is MIT. **D1:** reuse and adaptation of OSIRIS code that is actually MIT are allowed. Every licence, copyright and notice the MIT licence requires is kept; third-party code is never attributed to Alessandro Pezzali, who stays the sole author of NEXUM's original code; no false Co-authored-by in commits; MIT code is not re-implemented artificially only to avoid reuse. D1 covers **code only**: datasets, APIs, tiles, images, video, streams, assets and services still need their own audit (column 6).
- **REIMPLEMENT** — reproducible legally and at €0, better built in NEXUM's model (NEXUM already has, or should have, its own implementation in its Objects/Relations/Events/Evidence model).
- **ALTERNATIVE** — OSIRIS's provider fails the constraints, and a named, licensed, permanent-€0 equivalent exists.
- **IMPOSSIBLE** — documented evidence that no legal, sustainable, permanent-€0 way exists.
- **IMPOSSIBLE TODAY — RECHECK (D2)** — live aircraft: no legal, card-free, relay-free, permanently-€0 source today; the Cloudflare + ADSB.lol relay is **not approved**. Aircraft are not abandoned: the research continues. Rows whose code only serves live aircraft keep their class and are marked "blocked by D2".
- **IMPOSSIBLE TODAY / LEGAL CLARIFICATION REQUIRED (D4)** — abuse.ch (Feodo Tracker, URLhaus) is not used; the provider is not contacted.
- **Option A is superseded (D1).** The earlier column "Under Option A" (every REUSE/ADAPT row turned into REIMPLEMENT) is replaced by the column **"Final (D1 2026-10-07; D2, D4)"**. Tests P25 and W10 ("zero OSIRIS code") stay as they are until attributed MIT code is actually reused; at that point they are narrowed to allow exactly the attributed files and their notices.
- Mentions of "Option A" left inside the parts' own introductions and counts are historical (superseded by D1); the "Final" column and the totals above are authoritative.
- **Note on Part 2:** it marks REUSE wherever the MIT licence permits reuse; the other parts mark REIMPLEMENT where NEXUM already has, or should have, its own implementation. Under D1 the auditors' real classification is final for both readings.
- **GDACS (D6, final 2026-10-07):** direct access **SUSPENDED — LICENCE UNCONFIRMED / RECHECK**. NEXUM makes no request to gdacs.org; the 367 EONET events that mention GDACS are kept unchanged. Correction: the earlier statement that the GDACS terms page is a 404 came from a misspelled URL (`termsofuse.aspx`); the real page `gdacs.org/About/termofuse.aspx` answers 200 and holds disclaimers only, with no reuse grant or prohibition. Every GDACS page links "Copyright" to the European Commission legal notice (CC BY 4.0 for EU-owned content), which is not demonstrably applicable to the GDACS API and feeds: status UNCLEAR (evidence: `scratchpad/wave1/d6-gdacs.md`).

Part 5's rows are converted below into tables; their content is unchanged.

# Part 1 — Application shell and map foundation (OSIRIS 7a3daec → NEXUM)

Read-only audit, 2026-10-07. Every row below was verified in the code of both projects; the earlier NEXUM matrices were not used as evidence.

## Scope and method

**Licences.** All OSIRIS code is MIT, © 2026 simplifaisoul (`OSIRIS-REFERENCE/LICENSE`). The code could legally be reused with the notice kept. **Data, tile, API, asset and brand rights are separate** and are assessed per row. NEXUM's binding Option A (since 2026-09-28: zero OSIRIS code, assets or data, enforced by `tests/test_governance.py` P25) turns every REUSE or ADAPT row into "→ REIMPLEMENT under Option A".

**Terms checked live** (5 requests, neutral NEXUM UA, saved under `audit/terms/`):
- `raw.githubusercontent.com/CartoDB/basemap-styles/master/README.md`: *"An API key is required to use CARTO basemaps … free for non-commercial use up to 5M tile requests a month."*
- `ip-api.com/docs/legal`: *"strictly limited for a non-commercial purpose … Only members with a pro subscription can use the API in a commercial environment."*
- `operations.osmfoundation.org/policies/nominatim/`: at most 1 req/s, an identifying UA, visible attribution, results cached; *"Auto-complete search … you must not implement such a service on the client side using the API"*; reverse queries in a grid are forbidden.
- `openfreemap.org/tos/`: free, as-is; no automated scraping; may be discontinued.
- The TeleGeography README link returned 404, so the cable licence is not verified (see V-list). **Collision warning:** this request wrote to `audit/terms/tg.html`. If another auditor already had a file with that name, it was overwritten.

**OSIRIS files read** (path relative to `OSIRIS-REFERENCE/`):

- **App pages:** `src/app/page.tsx` (all 2004 lines), `src/app/layout.tsx`, `src/app/globals.css` (map overrides and responsive rules, l.320-510 and 2110-2140), `src/app/privacy/page.tsx`.
- **Docs pages:** `src/app/docs/page.tsx`, `DocsClient.tsx` (state, keys, nav), `CommandPalette.tsx` (keys), `EndpointCard.tsx` (actions), `apiCatalog.ts` (header).
- **Components:** `src/components/GlobalStatusBar.tsx`, `MapControls.tsx`, `KeyboardShortcuts.tsx`, `SearchBar.tsx`, `SharePanel.tsx`, `SupportMenu.tsx`, `TokenPanel.tsx`, `StyleStudio.tsx`, `ViewPresets.tsx`, `WorldRemote.tsx`, `ErrorBoundary.tsx`, `ScaleBar.tsx`.
- **`src/components/OsirisMap.tsx`, map-foundation parts only:** init, style, proxy, WebGL fallback (l.255-420); ready, mouse, context menu, view state, popup (l.895-975); generic click and satellite pick (l.1123-1200); ResizeObserver and day/night (l.1785-1812); visibility (l.2380-2440); projection, terrain, fly-to, 3D buildings, satellite style (l.2495-2680); follow and navigation camera (l.2900-2970); map-centre reporting and render (l.3236-3321).
- **`src/components/LayerPanel.tsx`, shell controls only:** DISPLAY group, Ghost toggle, Style Studio trigger, terrain status.
- **Libraries:** `src/lib/map-camera-controls.ts`, `map-projection.ts`, `map-terrain.ts`, `terrain-tiles.ts`, `terrain-layer-order.ts`, `map-tile-layout.ts`, `style-tokens.ts` (presets and storage), `map-palette.ts` (exports), `landing-cities.ts`, `countryCentroids.ts`, `geo.ts` (exports), `nominatim.ts` and `httpJson.ts` (UA and attribution lines).
- **API routes:** `src/app/api/geo/route.ts`, `geo/reverse/route.ts`, `geosearch/route.ts`, `proxy-tiles/route.ts`, `health/route.ts`, `stats/route.ts`, `github-webhook/route.ts`, `sdk/ingest/route.ts`, `sdk/stream/route.ts`.
- **SDK:** `src/lib/sdk/PolybolosClient.ts` and `LatticeAdapter.ts` (headers and usage).
- **Public assets:** `public/manifest.json`, `site.webmanifest`, `robots.txt`, `sitemap.xml`, `dark-matter-style.json`, `data/*`, `vendor/maplibre/6.7.0/`, and the icon and image inventory.
- **Tools:** `tools/prepare-map-worker.mjs`, `maplibre-url-loader.cjs`, `preview-smoke.mjs`; `package.json` and `next.config.ts` hooks.

**NEXUM files read** (relative to `NEXUM/ui/`):

- **Ops surface:** `src/ops/OpsShell.tsx`, `state.ts`, `basemaps.ts`, `theme.ts`, `geo.ts`, and `Panels.tsx` (ToolSheet, Layers, Style, Share, Help, Point, PlaceInput, `polite`).
- **Map:** `src/views/MapView.tsx` (init, controls, clicks), `src/map/points.ts` (clustering).
- **Shell:** `src/App.tsx` (bootstrap, keys), `src/components/StatusBar.tsx`, `Mobile.tsx`, `src/lib/layers.ts` (`DESK_W=1120`), `src/lib/update.ts` (service worker).
- **Config and assets:** `src/config/ops.json` (basemap, presets, geocode, privacy), `src/styles.css:145`, `web-public/manifest.webmanifest`, `scripts/assemble-web.mjs`, `dist-web/index.html` (meta).
- **Repository:** `tests/test_governance.py` (P25).

**Flag codes** (FLAGS column):

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| LV | Needs further legal verification |
| KEY | Needs a key or account |
| PROXY | Server proxy that relays or caches third-party content |
| SPOOF | Header spoofing (none found in this scope) |
| PAID | Paid tier or commercial restriction |
| DEMO | Demo or simulated endpoint |
| PRIV | Sends personal data, such as the visitor's IP, to a third party |
| POL | Conflicts with a NEXUM project constraint (no surveillance, no personal identifiers outbound) |

**Mobile.** OSIRIS switches to a phone layout below 768 px, or when the viewport is ≤500 px tall and <1024 px wide (`page.tsx:45-62`). CSS breakpoints are 1024, 768, 390 and landscape ≤500 px tall (`globals.css:434-490`). NEXUM switches to touch layouts below `DESK_W=1120` (`lib/layers.ts:9`). Each test criterion names the phone case where it applies.

## Needs legal verification

1. **CARTO basemaps (S32, S54, P05).** The README now requires a key, and OSIRIS proxies and caches tiles for a year. Confirm with `carto.com/legal/basemap-terms`. NEXUM must not use CARTO.
2. **Esri World Imagery (S31).** Production terms, account and attribution need confirming. Not to be used.
3. **IP geolocation providers (S04).** ipapi.co commercial limits and freeipapi.com ToS are unverified; ip-api.com is non-commercial (verified). The privacy conflict (visitor IP sent to third parties) applies regardless.
4. **Visible attribution in NEXUM (S33).** Confirm the exact required credit strings:
   - EOX: CC BY 4.0, 2016 mosaic; later years are CC BY-NC-SA.
   - OpenFreeMap and OpenMapTiles.
   - OSM: ODbL.
   - NASA GIBS.
   - AWS Terrain Tiles: per joerd `attribution.md`.
   - NOAA nowCOAST.
5. **Nominatim (S35, S91).** Autocomplete is forbidden and reverse lookups on hover are borderline. Photon's public-instance fair-use limits also need confirming.
6. **CoinGecko and other crypto price sources (S28, S23).** The keyless tier and attribution terms, and the terms of any exchange public ticker before use. The DexScreener embed terms are unverified.
7. **TeleGeography submarine cable data (P04).** The licence page returned 404, so it is unverified.
8. **MapLibre BSD-3 notice shipped in `dist-web` (S41, P06).**
9. **AWS Terrain Tiles attribution wording (S52).**

## Not covered

- Row S84 (rail REMOTE button) and the WorldRemote component (`src/components/WorldRemote.tsx`), plus the mobile REMOTE tab and drawer entry (page.tsx:1859-1869). These are left for a separate review.

# Part 1d — WorldRemote (classified by the coordinator; the sub-audit's writer was stopped by a safety filter on this topic)

| ID | OSIRIS CONTROL / CAPABILITY | COMPONENT / SOURCE FILE | WHAT IT REALLY DOES | DATA/API/SERVICE USED | LICENSE / TERMS / COST | NEXUM CURRENT EQUIVALENT | REAL FUNCTIONAL GAP | CLASSIFICATION | Final (D1 2026-10-07; D2, D4) | IMPLEMENTATION PLAN | TEST / ACCEPTANCE |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S84 | "World Remote" rail button, mobile REMOTE tab and drawer entry, and the WorldRemote panel | src/components/WorldRemote.tsx (header: "MARAUDER V8 — Browser-based BLE Recon Engine"); page.tsx 1859-1869 | Discovers and classifies Bluetooth devices near the person's device (phones, watches, audio, TVs, input devices), reads their exposed services and characteristics, and probes services on the local machine (localhost ports). Device reconnaissance, not open data. | Web Bluetooth API of the browser; requests to 127.0.0.1 | Code MIT. No data licence applies: the information is about third parties' devices nearby | none (deliberately) | NEXUM has no device reconnaissance | IMPOSSIBLE | **IMPOSSIBLE** | None. **Evidence:** NEXUM hard constraints (NEXUM-LEGAL-BOUNDARIES; memory "no surveillance"): no device intrusion, no surveillance or profiling of nearby devices or people, no local-network probing. Already declared IMPOSSIBLE in the 2026-10-04 matrix ("Bluetooth and device intrusion"). Not a cost or difficulty reason: it is excluded on legality, privacy and security grounds (it targets other people's devices without their consent). | P25/W10 governance keep OSIRIS code out; no Web Bluetooth or localhost probing anywhere in ui/src (grep check in the plan's test list). |

# Part 2: thematic map layers, space, aviation and environment (OSIRIS 7a3daec vs NEXUM main e2b17d0)

Audit date: 2026-10-07. Read-only. No OSIRIS or NEXUM files were changed. Network use was limited to licence and documentation pages and one GET per host, all sent with the User-Agent `NEXUM/0.1.0 (+https://github.com/pezzaliapp/NEXUM; local-first open-data research)`. No Origin header and no forged headers were sent.

## Intro

### OSIRIS files read (all under /Users/alessandropezzali/projects/OSIRIS-REFERENCE)
- `src/components/LayerPanel.tsx` (1-554, read in full)
- `src/components/OsirisMap.tsx`: 168, 300-470, 610-672, 792-900, 905-1226, 1584-1602, 1795-1990, 2360-2410, 2498-2660, 2963-3030. Every `addSource`/`addLayer`/`map.on` was listed with grep, and each thematic layer in scope was read.
- `src/components/SpaceCam.tsx` (full), `src/components/SatelliteCard.tsx` (full), `src/components/FlightWatchPanel.tsx` (full)
- `src/lib/orbit.ts` (full), `src/lib/satellite-layer.ts` (1-120, 250-330 plus the export list), `src/lib/satellite-programs.ts` (full), `src/lib/airports.ts` (header, exports and lookup functions), `src/lib/stealthFetch.ts` (header), `src/lib/terrain-tiles.ts` (host)
- `src/app/api/satellites/route.ts` (full), `src/app/api/satellites/orbit/route.ts` (full), `src/app/api/space-weather/route.ts` (full), `src/app/api/sentinel/route.ts` (full), `src/app/api/radar/route.ts` (full), `src/app/api/weather/route.ts` (1-30, 155-200), `src/app/api/air-quality/route.ts` (full), `src/app/api/fires/route.ts` (full), `src/app/api/earthquakes/route.ts` (full), `src/app/api/aircraft/route.ts` (full), `src/app/api/flights/route.ts` (full), `src/app/api/flight-route/route.ts` (full)
- `src/app/page.tsx` (190-275, 300-380, 640-880, 1355-1380, 1495-1510): layer defaults, fetch and poll wiring, Flight Watch, SpaceCam button
- `src/components/MarketsPanel.tsx` (203-240: where space weather is shown), `src/components/StyleStudio.tsx` (satellite swatches, 359-364)
- `LICENSE` (MIT, © 2026 simplifaisoul)

### NEXUM files read (all under /Users/alessandropezzali/projects/NEXUM)
`ui/src/ops/{state.ts, orbits.ts, orbits.worker.ts, overlays.ts (parts), alerts.ts (parts), basemaps.ts (parts), Sky.tsx (parts), Panels.tsx (layers, space, point, scenes, live, orbit card, feature card), OpsShell.tsx (Kp, quick toggles)}`, `ui/src/config/{ops.json, ops-space.json, imagery.json (head)}`, `ui/src/components/Imagery.tsx` (head and classes), `ui/src/map/illumination.ts` (head), `connectors/{celestrak_gp.py, firms_hotspots.py (hosts), noaa_swpc.py (head), ourairports_airports.py (head)}`, `sources_live/{celestrak.gp.toml, nasa.firms.toml}`, `.github/workflows/nexum-live.yml` (cadence: every 6 h).

### Evidence from the network (2026-10-07, one request per host)
| Host / endpoint | Status | CORS header seen | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| `api.adsb.lol/v2/mil` | 200 | none | Live aircraft JSON (readsb v2 shape). |
| `api.adsb.lol/api/openapi.json` (info block) | 200 | n/a | "You can use the API for free. In the future, you will require an API key which you can get by feeding to adsb.lol. If you want to use the API for production purposes, please contact me… The license for the API as well as all data ADSB.lol makes public is ODbL." |

A missing `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` on a request sent without an `Origin` header strongly suggests the browser will block the response, but it does not prove it. These must be confirmed from the NEXUM origin in a browser (see the legal list).

### Classification legend
REUSE / ADAPT / REIMPLEMENT / ALTERNATIVE / IMPOSSIBLE, as defined in the brief. NEXUM Option A copies no OSIRIS code, so REUSE and ADAPT become "REIMPLEMENT under Option A". OSIRIS code is MIT and the code itself is legally reusable. Every data, API or tile right is judged separately in each row.

### OSIRIS dead or non-functional code in this scope (nothing to reach)
- `balloons` and `radiation` layers: map sources and layers plus click handlers exist (OsirisMap.tsx:810-838, 1525-1557), but there is no `/api/balloons` or `/api/radiation` route and no LayerPanel toggle.
- `gps_jamming`: computed but never drawn (AV-06).
- The `NIGGG-BAS` earthquake link branch (OsirisMap.tsx:1116): no producer sets `source`.
- The watched-aircraft flown track: the comment says it is drawn, but only the airports are drawn (AV-11).
- `/api/earthquakes`, `/api/air-quality` (upstream 410), `/api/sentinel`, `/api/radar`: no UI consumer.
- The hidden `sat-glow` / `sat-dots` circle layers (OsirisMap.tsx:661-667).
- The hardcoded ISS emergency TLE from 2024 (satellites/route.ts:281-285) would draw a wildly wrong position today.

## Counts
- Matrix rows: **64** (LP 9, AV 17, SP 16, SC 7, SW 3, HZ 8, EN 3, XX 1)
- REUSE 39 · ADAPT 3 · REIMPLEMENT 1 · ALTERNATIVE 18 · IMPOSSIBLE 3
- Under Option A, REUSE and ADAPT become REIMPLEMENT: 43 rows to REIMPLEMENT under Option A (many already done in NEXUM), 18 ALTERNATIVE, 3 IMPOSSIBLE.

## Aircraft verdict (static €0 PWA, browser-direct)
- **OpenSky: no.** The CORS response header allows only `https://opensky-network.org` (probe). A meaningful quota needs an account with OAuth2 client credentials, whose secret cannot live in a public static bundle. Anonymous use is 400 credits/day per IP. The ToU page refused the fetcher (403), and the published REST docs and GitHub warn about blocking hyperscaler IPs.
- **adsb.fi: no.** Terms are "personal, non-commercial use only", 1 request per second, citation plus link required. A public website is not personal use. No CORS header was seen.
- **airplanes.live: no.** It answers 403 with "Please contact us…", so access is gated.
- **ADSB.lol: lawful, but not browser-direct.** Its data is **ODbL 1.0**: redistribution is allowed with attribution, and a derived database must be shared under ODbL. It is free today. The ToS says "In the future, you will require an API key which you can get by feeding to adsb.lol" and "for production purposes, please contact me". The code is BSD-3-Clause. Rate limits are "dynamic". No CORS header was seen, so the browser cannot read it from the NEXUM origin.
- **Result.** The only lawful €0 path is ADSB.lol through a NEXUM-operated relay on the edge's free tier (no card), viewport-bounded and cached, with ODbL credit and a kill switch. It is **not guaranteed permanent €0**, because the announced key requirement is tied to feeding and feeding needs receiver hardware. The 6-hour static snapshot is useless for aircraft.
- **NEXUM's current note is partly wrong.** The `ops.json orbits.airTraffic` note ("adsb.lol e adsb.fi … solo uso non commerciale") is inaccurate for ADSB.lol. ADSB.lol is ODbL, not non-commercial. The note should be corrected.

## Needs further legal verification (with reasons)
1. **OpenSky Network Terms of Use**: the page returned 403 to the fetcher. Confirm the non-commercial and research-only clauses and whether public website display is allowed, by reading the page in a browser.
2. **ADSB.lol production use**: the ToS asks "production" users to make contact. Confirm whether a public €0 relay counts as production. Get the timing of the API-key requirement in writing. Confirm what ODbL share-alike obliges for NEXUM's published aircraft-derived tables (AV-06 aggregates count as a derived database).
3. **CORS confirmation**: every "no ACAO" result came from a request without an `Origin` header. Run a real browser fetch from the NEXUM origin (live-review) for api.adsb.lol, opendata.adsb.fi and the IODA API.
4. **Privacy (GDPR) of ADS-B positions**: ADSB.lol does not state LADD/PIA filtering. Confirm NEXUM's privacy gate for GA and private-jet positions. AV-02 and AV-03 propose position-only output with no identity. Decide whether GA should be excluded entirely.
5. **Airline designator table** (AV-09 operator): find an openly licensed source (e.g. OpenFlights airlines.dat, ODbL, maintenance status unknown) and check its licence.
6. **adsbdb / PlaneBase aircraft data**: the licence is not stated. The route data is explicitly restricted, which supports AV-12 IMPOSSIBLE. hexdb.io terms are unknown and its endpoint returned 404.
7. **CelesTrak redistribution**: NEXUM's `sources_live/celestrak.gp.toml` says "allowed_with_attribution". Re-read the CelesTrak usage and data-use page, since the data derives from 18 SDS / Space-Track, whose user agreement restricts redistribution of its own downloads.
8. **SatNOGS DB licence** (CC BY-SA 4.0 assumed) for the SP-15 fallback.
9. **UCS Satellite Database terms** (SP-13): it is a free download with a citation request. Confirm that redistribution is allowed.
10. **Esri World Imagery** (HZ-08): confirm whether use outside ArcGIS needs an account. It only matters to justify not adopting it.
11. **CARTO basemaps** (HZ-06, only as OSIRIS context): free-tier limits and commercial terms.
12. **Open-Meteo**: "free for non-commercial use". Confirm NEXUM qualifies, and the daily call limit for a public site where each visitor calls directly.
13. **IODA terms** (EN-03) before any republication as NEXUM events.
14. **Edge relay versus the NEXUM model**: a Cloudflare Pages Function is free-plan and needs no card, but it changes the "static PWA plus snapshot" architecture. This is a project decision, not a legal one, and is needed before AV-01 to AV-17 can move.


---

# Part 3 — Alerts, news, intelligence feeds, AI, markets, supply chain, chain/crypto

Read-only audit, 2026-10-07. OSIRIS baseline: `/Users/alessandropezzali/projects/OSIRIS-REFERENCE` at commit `7a3daec` (MIT). NEXUM: `/Users/alessandropezzali/projects/NEXUM` at commit `e2b17d0`. Earlier NEXUM matrices were consulted only for orientation and are **not** relied on. Every NEXUM equivalence below was re-read in the source.

## Scope and method

**Exclusive scope:** Live Alerts and everything it depends on (digest, place resolution, the news route, the Telegram parser), Intel Feed, live TV news (layer, previews, YouTube helpers), AI overview, analyze and briefing (Gemini), the GDELT, GDELT-events, conflicts, frontlines, country-risk, region-dossier, entity and Cloudflare-Radar routes, Markets with its chart and history, crypto, the SCM panel and suppliers, and the chain brief, feeds and intel.

**Excluded (owned by other parts):** the map base; the space, aviation and environment layers; CCTV; maritime; infrastructure; routing and drawing; OSINT and cyber tools; ArcGIS import. Where an excluded producer feeds an in-scope consumer, only the consumption is audited. Examples are `/api/weather` feeding warnings and `/api/maritime` feeding `scm_alerts`.

**Network use:** one polite request per host, with User-Agent `NEXUM/0.1.0 (+https://github.com/pezzaliapp/NEXUM; local-first open-data research)` and no other headers. The fetched pages are saved under `scratchpad/audit/p3/`:
- **Read:** telegram.org/tos (redirected to /tos/eu), bbc.co.uk/news/10628494, theguardian.com/help/terms-of-service, legal.yahoo.com OTOS, ai.google.dev/gemini-api/terms, deepstatemap.live/en, gdeltproject.org/about.html, opensanctions.org/licensing, developers.cloudflare.com/radar, apidoc.reliefweb.int, nvd.nist.gov/developers/terms-of-use, developers.google.com/youtube/terms/developer-policies, aljazeera.com/terms-and-conditions, and the lightweight-charts LICENSE.
- **Refused us (403):** coingecko.com/en/api_terms, coinbase.com developer ToS, defillama.com/docs/api.
- **404:** gdacs.org/About/termsofuse.aspx. *(Correction 2026-10-07: misspelled URL; the real page `gdacs.org/About/termofuse.aspx` answers 200, disclaimers only — see D6 above.)*
- **429:** one GDELT DOC API probe (`api.gdeltproject.org/api/v2/doc/doc`) returned 429 with "Please limit requests to one every 5 seconds", and no CORS header was observed.

### NEXUM files read
- `ui/src/ops/alerts.ts` (1–111), `ui/src/ops/Ai.tsx` (1–87)

- `connectors/gdelt_events.py` (1–110), `connectors/commodities.py` (URLs), `connectors/registers.py` (URLs)
- `sources_live/gdelt.events.toml`, `ucdp.candidate.toml`, `wikidata.heads.toml`, `us.ofac_sdn.toml` and `commodities.toml`
- `.github/workflows/nexum-live.yml` (cron), `NEXUM-LEGAL-BOUNDARIES.md` (V5 and lines 18, 39, 72, 78, 87–103), and `NEXUM-SOURCES-LIVE.md` (GDACS line 79)

### Structural facts that change the reading of OSIRIS
1. **Dead or unwired OSIRIS code.**
   - `ScmPanel` is imported (`page.tsx:11`) but never rendered, and nothing fills `data.scm_suppliers`.
   - `/api/scm-suppliers`, `/api/frontlines`, `/api/country-risk`, `/api/entity/expand`, `/api/crypto`, `/api/ai/analyze` and `/api/ai/briefing` are listed in `src/app/docs/apiCatalog.ts`, but no UI component calls them.
   - They are still audited as capabilities.
2. **OSIRIS sends forged browser User-Agents** to news sites, RSS wires and Yahoo:
   - `news/route.ts:87,177,218`
   - `conflicts/route.ts:163,187-189`
   - `markets/route.ts:67,79` and `markets/history/route.ts:38,88`

   NEXUM forbids this (`NEXUM-LEGAL-BOUNDARIES.md:72`, "User-Agent onesto… Mai user-agent… di browser simulati"). No such fetch can be ported as it is.
3. **OSIRIS invents some positions and times.** `/api/conflicts` places RSS headlines at an editorial zone centroid plus a deterministic offset, and stamps each with the fetch time (`conflicts/route.ts:240-257`). The route also says "GDELT" in its header while reading BBC, Al Jazeera and NYT RSS (`:10-14` vs `:151-155`).
4. **OSIRIS treats nearness as risk.** `scm-suppliers/route.ts:47-85` sets CRITICAL for a quake within 150 km and HIGH for a fire within 50 km. It also labels GDACS disasters "ARMED CONFLICT / RIOT" (`:75-84`), and `markets/route.ts:205-207` prints causal price claims. NEXUM rules forbid presenting nearness as cause or risk.
5. **NEXUM news is a static snapshot.**
   - `/tables/newsevents` is rebuilt by the cron `17 */6 * * *` (`.github/workflows/nexum-live.yml:14`), and the table holds only the four 15-minute GDELT slots before build time (`connectors/gdelt_events.py:26,33-38`). So "Aggiorna" re-reads a table that can be up to about 6 h old and covers a 1-hour window.
   - OSIRIS re-reads its channels every 3 minutes over a 72-hour window (`news/route.ts:83-86`).
6. **The NEXUM AI runs in the browser.** WebLLM loads Qwen2.5 0.5B or 1.5B on demand (`Ai.tsx:10-13,45-52`), with no key, server or cost. It only rewrites the facts of the element in focus (`Ai.tsx:15-35`). It has **no alerts, markets or chain brief mode**.

## Classification legend
- **REUSE:** OSIRIS code is usable directly; it is MIT and dependency-free.
- **ADAPT:** OSIRIS code is usable with adaptation.
- **REIMPLEMENT:** legal at €0, and better built natively in NEXUM's Objects, Relations, Events, Evidence and WHY.
- **ALTERNATIVE:** the OSIRIS provider fails NEXUM constraints, and a named legal permanent-€0 replacement exists.
- **IMPOSSIBLE:** documented evidence attached.

Under **Option A** (zero OSIRIS code), REUSE and ADAPT become "REIMPLEMENT under Option A".

Abbreviations used in the table: LA = `src/components/LiveAlerts.tsx`, AD = `src/lib/alert-digest.ts`, AP = `src/lib/alert-places.ts`, NR = `src/app/api/news/route.ts`, OM = `src/components/OsirisMap.tsx`, PG = `src/app/page.tsx`, NP = `ui/src/ops/Panels.tsx`, NA = `ui/src/ops/alerts.ts`, NO = `ui/src/config/ops.json`, LB = `NEXUM-LEGAL-BOUNDARIES.md`.

**How the plan column handles live items.** Every plan that touches live reports follows one NEXUM pattern:
- A live media item becomes a **reported Event**, "riportato", never "verificato".
- Its articles become **Evidence**.
- Its place becomes a **Relation** to a Place Object, carrying a precision attribute.
- **WHY** ("Perché?") lists the rule and its inputs.
- Distance relations are labelled as distance only, never as risk or cause.

## Counts

135 rows: REUSE 8 · ADAPT 54 · REIMPLEMENT 43 · ALTERNATIVE 21 · IMPOSSIBLE 9 (P3-029, 030, 049, 095, 100, 104, 107, 123, 135). Under Option A: 62 "REIMPLEMENT under Option A" (ex REUSE+ADAPT) + 43 REIMPLEMENT + 21 ALTERNATIVE + 9 IMPOSSIBLE.

## Top functional gaps

1. **News freshness and window (P3-004, P3-054).** NEXUM's "Allerte dal vivo" news comes from a 1-hour GDELT window frozen at each 6-hourly snapshot. OSIRIS reads its sources every 3–5 minutes over a 72-hour window.
2. **No real headlines, summaries or outlet names (P3-027, P3-031, P3-026, P3-032).** Items are titled by the translated CAMEO code ("Combattimenti"), and the source is "GDELT". The licensed path is GDELT GKG `PAGE_TITLE`, plus publisher syndication only where its terms allow.
3. **The map does not follow the panel's filters (P3-042, P3-043, P3-044).** Live items have no WHY or Evidence (P3-056) and no precision label (P3-034, P3-035), so GDELT country centroids look like event locations.
4. **No structured clustering, perspective or brief (P3-016, P3-017, P3-062, P3-064, P3-080, P3-084).** There are no threads, no outlet-country perspective, no deterministic Sintesi, and no alerts, markets or chain mode for the local AI.
5. **Warnings are thin (P3-023, P3-038, P3-039).**
   - No expiry countdown, urgency or certainty is shown.
   - There is no severity-first tab.
   - No official warning outside the US is licensed: GDACS reuse is still unlicensed.
6. **Live TV directory (P3-040, P3-073, P3-074).** NEXUM has 7 news channels against OSIRIS's 22, with no map placement, no region grouping and no search.
7. **Markets depth (P3-103, P3-105, P3-108, P3-110, P3-112–117, P3-121, P3-122).**
   - Missing: section tabs, a dollar index (Fed H.10), daily gas (EIA), XRP, day-change on FX, breadth, sort, an interactive chart and crypto intraday.
   - Equities, indices, VIX and intraday data for non-crypto series are IMPOSSIBLE.
8. **Chain and supply chain (P3-126–P3-133).** Missing: a window, totals, crypto CVEs (NVD) and OFAC digital-currency addresses taken from the official SDN, plus a supply-chain view whose distance relations are labelled distance-only. The proximity-as-risk rules are not reproduced.

## Needs further legal verification

1. **Gemini.** The EEA paid-only clause is confirmed, so Gemini is excluded. Still to confirm: the gemini-2.0-flash deprecation date, if it is ever reconsidered for a user-supplied key (LB:78).
2. **Headlines (GDELT GKG `PAGE_TITLE`).** Can third-party headlines be reproduced in NEXUM? Check the EU DSM Directive art. 15 press-publishers' right and its "very short extracts" exception, and its Italian implementation (L. 633/1941 art. 43-bis).
3. **BBC News feeds.** The feed page allows website use with attribution. Check the full BBC Terms of Use for redistribution through a static snapshot.
4. **The Guardian.** Read the RSS and Open Platform terms; the help ToS only mentions RSS as an access channel.
5. **Other wires.** The terms of TASS, Anadolu, SCMP, CNA, Africanews and Times of Israel were not read. Al Jazeera's terms ("personal, non-commercial use") were read and exclude it.
6. **Telegram.** Read the referenced "Content Licensing and AI Scraping Terms" page in full, for the record.
7. **UN News, EU Commission press corner and US government press feeds.** Confirm the reuse licences (Decision 2011/833/EU for the EU; public domain for the US) and the terms of each feed.
8. **GDACS.** *(Corrected: the 404 was a misspelled URL; `termofuse.aspx` answers 200 with disclaimers only; the "Copyright" link leads to the EC legal notice, CC BY 4.0 for EU-owned content — applicability to the API/feeds UNCLEAR, D6.)* Confirm its current terms. NEXUM-SOURCES-LIVE.md:79 says there is no reuse permission, yet live display continues.
9. **DeepState.** Confirm that no licence or terms exist, and ask the owners. Also check whether ISW/CTP map layers carry a reusable licence.
10. **Internet outages.** Cloudflare Radar data is CC BY-NC 4.0 and needs a token: check NC compatibility with NEXUM's non-commercial publication. Check the IODA API terms and data licence as the keyless alternative.
11. **CoinGecko.** The API terms returned 403, and it is unclear whether the keyless tier still exists.
12. **Coinbase.** The developer-platform ToS returned 403. Check the public exchange-rates endpoint and the Exchange candles endpoint for display and redistribution.
13. **DefiLlama.** The API docs returned 403. Check the API terms and data licence (code is GPL; data terms unclear).
14. **NVD.** Check the keyless rate limit and the terms text; the page was fetched but the text was not matched.
15. **OpenSanctions.** CC BY-NC 4.0 is confirmed, so it is excluded. Confirm that OFAC's official SDN data carries "Digital Currency Address" IDs in the free CSV/XML.
16. **National CAP warning feeds.** Check the licence of each one (DWD, MeteoSwiss, Met Éireann, AEMET, Meteo Italia/DPC) and of MeteoAlarm.
17. **Federal Reserve H.10.** Check the reuse terms and the DDP download policy.
18. **FRED notices.** FRED's copyright notices on S&P 500, Nasdaq and VIX series support the IMPOSSIBLE verdict; quote them verbatim.
19. **Exchange calendars.** Find the reuse status of exchange holiday calendars and official session hours.
20. **YouTube.** Confirm that each news channel permits embedding, and check ePrivacy consent wording for loading youtube-nocookie in the EU (NEXUM already loads it on tap only).
21. **Wikipedia extracts.** Check the CC BY-SA 4.0 attribution and share-alike obligations for extracts shown in the dossier.
22. **Photon.** Check komoot's fair-use policy for reverse geocoding on user tap.
23. **GeoNames.** Check CC BY 4.0 attribution for an offline gazetteer.
24. **Refresh cadence.** Check GitHub Actions and Pages terms, and Cloudflare Pages Free deploy limits (builds per month), for a 15-minute `newsevents` refresh.
25. **lightweight-charts.** Check whether a NOTICE file applies and whether the TradingView attribution logo or link is required by default.
26. **Qwen2.5 and Hugging Face.** Confirm the Qwen2.5-0.5B/1.5B-Instruct model-card licence (Apache-2.0) and the Hugging Face terms for downloading the mlc-ai weights from the browser.
27. **GDELT DOC API.** It returned 429 to a single probe ("one every 5 seconds") with no CORS header seen. Confirm the policy before any browser-side use; GKG or export files built by the snapshot are preferred.

# Part 4 — Routing / navigation, drawing / measurement / AOI / watch, geocoding, maritime, infrastructure, dataset discovery & import

Audit date: 2026-10-07 · read-only · OSIRIS baseline `/Users/alessandropezzali/projects/OSIRIS-REFERENCE` @ `7a3daec` (MIT) · NEXUM `/Users/alessandropezzali/projects/NEXUM` @ `e2b17d0` (working tree clean).
Earlier NEXUM matrices were NOT used; every row below was re-derived from the source files listed here.

## 0. Method and files read

### OSIRIS files read in full (line numbers below refer to these)

- `src/app/api/geo/route.ts` (grep of providers, lines 22–80) — IP geolocation fallback used by "Use my location".
- `src/lib/nominatim.ts` (207) — server-side Nominatim gate/cache.
- `src/lib/httpJson.ts` (UA lines 14–45).
- `src/components/DrawHud.tsx` (134), `src/components/DrawingToolbar.tsx` (493), `src/lib/draw.ts` (255), `src/lib/aoi.ts` (176), `src/lib/aoi-export.ts` (150), `src/lib/watch.ts` (136), `src/lib/geo.ts` (lines 1–120).
- `src/app/api/maritime/route.ts` (347) + `route.test.ts` (74, skimmed).
- `src/app/api/infrastructure/route.ts` (157) + `netherlands.test.ts` (54, skimmed).
- `src/components/ArcGISPanel.tsx` (625), `src/app/api/arcgis/route.ts` (343) + `route.test.ts` (88, skimmed), `src/lib/ssrf-guard.ts` (`isRateLimited` 221+).
- `src/components/OsirisMap.tsx` (3321) — in-scope sections only: infra layers 630–652, maritime/choke 673–705, ships 888–899, click handlers 1558–1700, setGeo 2172–2180, visibility 2395–2398, drawn polygons 2654–2710, directions route 2712–2813, route framing 2815–2833, live user location 2835–2915, follow / heading-up 2916–2965, ArcGIS layers 3032–3084, drawing mode 3091–3240.
- `src/app/page.tsx` — wiring only: geolocation watch 279–293, ArcGIS state 295, AOI persistence 590–596, tripwire sweep 598–617, GeoJSON export 646, maritime/infra polling 771/796/878–890, NavigationView + reroute 1298–1325, ArcGIS panel wiring 1613–1624.

### NEXUM files read
`ui/src/ops/Panels.tsx` (helpers 106–160, DrawPanel/ShapeRow 235–340, RoutePanel/PlaceInput/Elevation/Navigate 536–756, ImportPanel 1020–1112, ChokeDaily/PortDaily 1239–1275, FeatCard maritime/imported 1359–1450), `ui/src/ops/draw.ts` (134), `ui/src/ops/geo.ts` (exports), `ui/src/ops/overlays.ts` (310), `ui/src/ops/SeaRoutePanel.tsx` (173), `ui/src/ops/searoute.ts` (80), `ui/src/ops/searouteLayer.ts` (36), `ui/src/ops/OpsShell.tsx` (load 51–124, MaritimeGroup 275–325, shortcuts 398–419), `ui/src/config/ops.json` (sections geocode 415, route 426, ais 460, arcgis 473, cables 789, card 868, portwatch 890, routes 899), `connectors/osm_overpass.py`, `connectors/searoute_marnet.py`, `connectors/wri_power_plants.py` (46–52), `sources_live/wri.power_plants.toml`, `nga.world_port_index.toml`, `osm.navalbases.toml`, `eurostat.searoute.toml`.

### Terms / policy pages fetched (one polite request each, UA `NEXUM/0.1.0 (+https://github.com/pezzaliapp/NEXUM; local-first open-data research)`, saved under `scratchpad/audit/pages/`)
operations.osmfoundation.org/policies/nominatim · routing.openstreetmap.de/about.html · fossgis.de/arbeitsgruppen/osm-server/nutzungsbedingungen · Project-OSRM wiki "Api-usage-policy" · photon.komoot.io · graphhopper.com/pricing · openrouteservice.org/plans (SPA, no text returned) · aisstream.io + /documentation · digitraffic.fi/en/terms-of-service · ip-api.com/docs/legal · esri.com legal data-attributions · imf.org/external/terms.htm (empty shell) · one probe each: valhalla1.openstreetmap.de/status (200, Valhalla 3.9.1), arcgis.com/sharing/rest/search (200, 1 result).

### Conventions
- **Data-nature tags for maritime:** `OBSERVED-AIS` (a position a ship's transponder broadcast, received by a terrestrial/satellite station), `REPORTED` (a figure a third party computed/declared — e.g. IMF PortWatch counts derived from AIS, TEU volumes, fleet names), `MODELLED` (computed by a model/heuristic — sea route, "congestion", "risk"), `STATIC` (a fixed infrastructure fact — port, base, chokepoint location).
- **Classification** exactly one of REUSE / ADAPT / REIMPLEMENT / ALTERNATIVE / IMPOSSIBLE as defined in the brief. Under **NEXUM Option A** (zero OSIRIS code copied — which is the project's own constraint "zero OSIRIS"), every REUSE/ADAPT becomes "REIMPLEMENT under Option A".
- **Layer orthogonality rules applied in every plan:** each tool owns its own `source`/`layer`/handler ids (prefix shown), never sets another layer's visibility/opacity/filter/interaction, and on close removes only its own sources, layers and handlers; nothing heavy is rendered globally — data loads on request.
- "Mobile" acceptance = 390×844 CSS px viewport, touch only, bottom sheet; every criterion is checkable by a Playwright test or a hand test.

## 1. Routing / geocoding endpoints — verdict (quoted terms)

| Endpoint | Who uses it | What the operator's policy says (quoted) | Verdict for NEXUM |
|---|---|---|---|
| `router.project-osrm.org` (OSRM demo) | OSIRIS fallback `directions/route.ts:22,315-324` | Policy page now redirects: "Please see https://routing.openstreetmap.de/about.html and … fossgis.de … for details of access to the current demo server." Legacy rules "still good practice": "Access to the Demo Server shall be withdrawn at any time and without giving a reason." "We don't give any quality guarantees." Driving profile only. | Not infrastructure. Best-effort demo; no walking/cycling. Do not adopt. |
| `valhalla1.openstreetmap.de` (FOSSGIS Valhalla) | OSIRIS primary `route.ts:20-21`; NEXUM primary `ops.json:443`, `Panels.tsx:619-625` | FOSSGIS terms (EN version): "OpenStreetMap data can be used freely and at no cost by anyone. Our services, however, cannot." "Routing servers … Maximum of one request per second." "Commercial use is only permitted if the use of the services does not constitute a substantial part of an online offering." "Websites with high traffic volume are generally not allowed to use our services." "For websites, an email address of the operator must be easily identifiable and directly reachable." "Explicitly recommended: The URLs of our services should not be hardcoded into the app." "We reserve the right to discontinue services at any time without giving reasons or advance notice." "We do not guarantee any availability." | Usable only as a **best-effort, on-request, low-traffic, non-commercial** service, with attribution + fixthemap link (NEXUM has both) and **an operator contact e-mail on the site (NEXUM: not found — legal gap)**. The 1 req/s limit is per application, not per browser: NEXUM's `polite()` gate (`Panels.tsx:120-125`) is per tab, so N concurrent users can exceed it. URLs are bundled in `ops.json` (hard-coded at build) — against the explicit recommendation. **Not a guaranteed €0 product infrastructure.** |
| `routing.openstreetmap.de` (FOSSGIS OSRM car/bike/foot) | NEXUM fallback `ops.json:427`, `Panels.tsx:631-637` | Same FOSSGIS terms; about page: "Display the required attribution and display a link to 'fix the map'. Use a valid user agent and, if applicable, a correct referrer. One request per second max. No scraping, no heavy usage." Requests are "saved in the server log file". | Same verdict as Valhalla (same operator, same budget). Advantage over OSIRIS: bike and foot fallback exist. |
| `valhalla1.openstreetmap.de/height` (elevation) | OSIRIS `route.ts:343`; NEXUM `Panels.tsx:590` | Covered by the same FOSSGIS routing terms (1 req/s). | Same; one extra call per walk/bike route — counts against the same per-app budget. |
| `photon.komoot.io` | OSIRIS `geosearch/route.ts:25,145-151` (server); NEXUM `ops.json:416`, `Panels.tsx:546-547` (browser) | "You can use the API for your project, but please be fair - extensive usage will be throttled. We do not guarantee for the availability and usage might be subject of change in the future." | Fair-use, no SLA. Type-ahead is its intended use ("search-as-you-type"). Acceptable on request with debounce; not guaranteed. |
| `nominatim.openstreetmap.org` | OSIRIS `nominatim.ts:34`, used as **type-ahead supplement** `geosearch/route.ts:153-156,179-185`; NEXUM: URL in `ops.json:417` but **not called anywhere in `ui/src`** | "No heavy uses (an absolute maximum of 1 request per second)." "the usage limits above apply per website/application". "Apps must make sure that they can switch the service at our request at any time (… without requiring a software update)." **Unacceptable use: "Auto-complete search — This is not yet supported by Nominatim and you must not implement such a service on the client side using the API."** "Systematic queries … reverse queries in a grid … are strictly forbidden". | OSIRIS's use (Nominatim queried from a debounced keystroke search box whenever Photon returns < 3 hits) is **auto-complete through Nominatim — forbidden by the policy**, mitigated but not cured by the server gate. NEXUM must keep Nominatim out of the type-ahead; at most an explicit "search" button submit, ≤1 req/s per application, cached. |
| OpenRouteService (HeiGIT) | neither | `openrouteservice.org/plans` returned an SPA shell (no text). Requires an account/API key (README: public "live API" separate from the self-hostable software). | Key + quota → not permanent €0 by construction (account, revocable quota). Software itself (GPL-3.0? — verify) is self-hostable. Needs legal verification (list §4). |
| GraphHopper Directions API | neither | Pricing page: free plan "0 € … 500 per day … Max. locations per request 5"; "The Free Plan is for non-commercial use only." "No credit card required." | Key + 500 credits/day + non-commercial → quota/metered risk; not adopted. |
| IP geolocation (ipapi.co / freeipapi.com / ip-api.com) | OSIRIS `api/geo/route.ts:22-80` via "Use my location" fallback `DirectionsBar.tsx:566-577` | ip-api.com: "The use of the API is strictly limited for a non-commercial purpose and in a non-commercial environment." "45 requests per minute". OSIRIS calls it over plain HTTP and sends the visitor's IP to three third parties. | Rejected (privacy: visitor IP to third parties; non-commercial-only). Use the W3C Geolocation API only (already used by NEXUM navigation). |

**€0 sustainable alternative (named, with evidence):**
1. *Short term (today):* keep FOSSGIS Valhalla → FOSSGIS OSRM as **best-effort** providers, but (a) move provider URLs out of the build into a runtime-fetched `providers.json` (switchable without a software update — Nominatim and FOSSGIS both ask for this), (b) add an application-wide budget signal (the static site cannot coordinate tabs; so cap per-device at 1 req/s *and* refuse automatic/periodic routing — routing only on explicit "Calcola" and on reroute ≥20 s apart, which NEXUM already does), (c) publish an operator contact address on the site (FOSSGIS requirement — see legal list; must not be a personal e-mail per the project rule, so a project mailbox/alias is needed), (d) say plainly in the UI "servizio comunitario senza garanzie" (already in `ops.json:426` credit).
2. *Permanent €0 path:* **self-hosted engine on the person's own machine** — Valhalla (MIT licence) or OSRM (BSD-2-Clause) run locally against a Geofabrik regional OSM extract (ODbL), exposed at `http://localhost:8002` / `:5000`; NEXUM's `providers.json` gets a "use my own routing server" field. Costs €0 in licences; the cost is the user's own hardware. Same for geocoding: Photon (Apache-2.0) or Nominatim self-hosted.
3. *Fully in-browser (zero server) for limited scope:* the sea-route approach already proves in-page Dijkstra over a published static graph (`searoute.ts`). For roads this is only feasible for a small region (city/province graph published as a static table); global road routing in-browser is not feasible — documented limit, not a reason to drop the online path.

## 3. Separate note — military / cyber / dual-use themes in dataset discovery (legality, privacy, security)

1. **What OSIRIS does:** a one-tap preset "Military → `military base installation`" (`ArcGISPanel.tsx:65`) plus "Pipelines", "Power Grid", "Critical Infrastructure" — and imports *any* public Feature Service with no licence check (`arcgis/route.ts:252-336`). The OSIRIS AOI also sweeps "Military aircraft" (`aoi.ts:68`) and its tripwire logs identifiable aircraft/vessels entering an area (`watch.ts`).
2. **Legality of the data:** "public" on ArcGIS Online means *access* is public, not that reuse is licensed. The live probe (§0) shows a pipeline layer licensed under the "Esri Master License Agreement" — proprietary terms. Importing/redistributing such layers outside the publisher's terms is not covered; NEXUM's current "licenseInfo non-empty" gate does not catch it (X-11). Many governments publish critical-infrastructure layers deliberately (e.g. US EIA, CC-like/public domain) — these are lawful to view and reuse with attribution.
3. **Security / dual-use:** aggregating critical-infrastructure geometry (pipelines, substations, cables, military sites) with live movement data is a classic dual-use concern. Several jurisdictions restrict publication of critical-infrastructure protection information (e.g. EU Directive 2022/2557 on critical entities resilience leaves protective information to member states; Italy's rules on "infrastrutture critiche" and on military zones — **needs legal verification**, §4). Mapping military installations as named in public sources is generally lawful; adding order-of-battle (which fleet is where, live military vessel/aircraft positions) is what OSIRIS does and NEXUM does not.
4. **Privacy:** imported layers can contain personal data (addresses of shelters' managers, cameras, owners). NEXUM keeps imports in the browser only (good) but the FeatCard shows *all* attributes once X-18 is implemented — so a field-name filter for obvious personal fields (`email`, `phone`, `owner_name`, `cf`, `address` of persons) should hide them by default.
5. **NEXUM position (keep):** no presets for military installations, persons, or surveillance cameras (`ops.json:503` gate); free-text search remains open; import only for **open-licensed** items (after X-11 fix); naval bases only as name+operator from OSM; ships only commercial classes, Baltic, no track history; watch only on events and aggregate counts (W-03/W-05), never identities (W-02 IMPOSSIBLE). Cyber datasets (C2 servers, scanners) are out of this part's scope but the same rule applies: no live attribution of identifiable hosts from a discovery tool.

## 4. Needs further legal verification

1. **FOSSGIS operator-contact requirement** — terms: "For websites, an email address of the operator must be easily identifiable and directly reachable." NEXUM shows none; the project rule forbids personal identifiers outbound — a project alias/contact page must be decided by the owner.
2. **FOSSGIS "high traffic" and commercial clauses** — whether NEXUM's expected traffic and any future commercial context fit "keinen wesentlichen Teil eines Onlineangebots" / "Webseiten mit hohem Trafficaufkommen dürfen … grundsätzlich nicht".
3. **Hard-coded provider URLs** — FOSSGIS ("should not be hardcoded") and Nominatim ("switch … without requiring a software update"): is a static redeploy acceptable, or must `providers.json` be fetched at runtime?
4. **OpenRouteService public API terms** — plans page returned no text (SPA); free-plan commercial/non-commercial status and quota not verified.
5. **aisstream.io redistribution terms** — `/terms` returned 404; docs forbid browser connections and give no SLA; whether positions may be republished in a public app is undocumented.
6. **Norwegian Coastal Administration (Kystverket) open AIS** — licence (NLOD 2.0 expected) and permitted real-time redisplay; not fetched in this audit.
7. **IMF PortWatch reuse terms** — `imf.org/external/terms.htm` returned an empty shell; NEXUM's credit claims "riuso consentito con attribuzione"; confirm the exact IMF copyright/usage clause for PortWatch data and the ArcGIS-hosted endpoints (services9.arcgis.com).
8. **Esri ArcGIS Online anonymous API use** — whether anonymous programmatic use of `/sharing/rest/search` and of public hosted services by a third-party app is within Esri's Terms of Use; and how the "Esri Master License Agreement" licence on public items restricts viewing vs reuse.
9. **IAEA PRIS** — terms for reuse of reactor lists/coordinates (if chosen over Wikidata for I-01).
10. **Photon (komoot)** — "be fair" has no number; confirm acceptable request volume for a public PWA type-ahead.
11. **Critical-infrastructure / military-zone publication rules in Italy and the EU** (Directive (EU) 2022/2557 implementation; Italian rules on military areas/photography) for any layer combining infrastructure geometry with live movement.
12. **MMSI→flag derivation** (M-12) — the ITU MID table is factual; confirm no licence restriction on redistributing the MID list.
13. **OSRM demo (`router.project-osrm.org`)** — now points to FOSSGIS; confirm whether the host is still separately operated and under which terms (only relevant if anyone proposes it).

## 5. Counts

124 matrix rows (R 31 · N 14 · G 6 · D 16 · A 4 · W 5 · M 19 · I 6 · X 23).
Classification: REUSE 0 · ADAPT 4 (R-16, G-01, X-03, X-08) · REIMPLEMENT 106 · ALTERNATIVE 11 (R-09, R-17, G-02, M-01, M-04, M-05, M-06, M-07, M-08, M-19, I-01) · IMPOSSIBLE 3 (G-03 cross-user Nominatim budget in a static PWA; W-02 identity tripwire on vehicles — NEXUM "no surveillance" constraint; M-17 empty OSIRIS satellite-AIS stub).
Under Option A: REIMPLEMENT 110 · ALTERNATIVE 11 · IMPOSSIBLE 3.

Top gaps (priority): (1) X-11 licence gate is nominal (Esri-MLA items importable); (2) N-02/N-03 navigation uses nearest-maneuver and vertex distance (wrong step after a turn, false off-route on straights); (3) R-31/R-10/R-11/N-10 no position dot, tracking or follow camera on the map; (4) §1 routing on FOSSGIS without operator contact, with bundled URLs and per-tab-only rate limit; (5) A-01 AOI "what is inside" ignores the operational overlays (ports, chokepoints, AIS, cables, naval, imports); (6) X-10 ArcGIS layer discovery takes layers[0] (group/tile-only failures) and X-08 only services*.arcgis.com; (7) D-04 no draw keyboard and draft possibly left armed after tool close; (8) R-01 route layers not removed on close; (9) I-01 no nuclear-specific view and no non-power nuclear sites; (10) M-01 observed AIS only in the Baltic (global free AIS not available under open terms without a server).

# NEXUM ⇄ OSIRIS Parity Audit — Part 5: OSINT, Cyber & Security Tooling, Intel Server, Backend Plumbing

Read-only audit. OSIRIS-REFERENCE @ 7a3daec (MIT Next.js app). NEXUM @ main e2b17d0 (€0 static PWA + static snapshot). Scope (exclusive): the OSINT recon toolkit, cyber/security tooling, the intel server (`intel/server.js`), the agent engine (`engine/`), and backend plumbing (ssrf-guard, stealthFetch, client-ip, httpJson, fetch-pool, sourceCache, osint-utils, github-webhook, health, stats). EXCLUDED: map shell, layers, CCTV, maritime, routing, alerts/news/markets.

All HTTP probes used the project UA `NEXUM/0.1.0 (+https://github.com/pezzaliapp/NEXUM; local-first open-data research)`, one polite GET per host, no forged headers, no personal data, no scanners/lookups against real targets.

---

## OSIRIS files read (exhaustive, this scope)

Components / client libs:
- `src/components/OsintPanel.tsx` (1644 lines — 19 tools, groups, self-track, sweep, fullscreen, result renderers)
- `src/components/FingerprintSearch.tsx` (923 lines — username/email/phone unified search, streaming, CSV/JSON export)
- `src/lib/fingerprint.ts` (181 — query validation, platform categories, NDJSON framing, CSV export, history)
- `src/lib/sherlock.ts` (418 — username enumeration engine, Sherlock data.json reimplementation, control-handle calibration)
- `src/lib/sanctions.ts` (255 — OFAC SDN via OpenSanctions CSV, normalisation, search/matchExact)
- `src/lib/c2-indicators.ts` (124 — abuse.ch Feodo Tracker C2 blocklist parser)
- `src/lib/malware-intel.ts` (380 — pure URLhaus parsing/geo-join/beacons)
- `src/lib/malware-live.ts` (511 — live URLhaus poll loop + ip-api batch geolocation + SSE store)
- `src/lib/ssrf-guard.ts` (312 — validateHost, safeFetch, isRateLimited, getClientIp, isPublicIp, visitorIp)
- `src/lib/stealthFetch.ts` (117 — forged residential IP + browser-UA header generator)
- `src/lib/httpJson.ts` (141 — Node https client, honest OSIRIS_UA, conditional GET)
- `src/lib/fetch-pool.ts` (47 — bounded concurrency pool)
- `src/lib/sourceCache.ts` (132 — TTL + dedup + stale-on-error index cache)
- `src/lib/osint-utils.ts` (185 — IPv4 parse, subnet math, device classification, risk, batchFetch)
- `src/lib/client-ip.test.ts` (only client-ip artefact present; logic lives in ssrf-guard.ts)
- `src/lib/sdk/LatticeAdapter.ts`, `PolybolosClient.ts`, `types.ts` (headers scanned)

API routes:
- `src/app/api/scanner/route.ts` (98), `src/app/api/osint/sweep/route.ts` (126), `src/app/api/osint/shodan/route.ts` (38), `src/app/api/osint/username/route.ts` (66), `src/app/api/osint/fingerprint/route.ts` (88), `src/app/api/osint/whois/route.ts` (115), `src/app/api/osint/threats/route.ts` (105), `src/app/api/osint/sanctions/route.ts` (65), `src/app/api/osint/dns/route.ts` (66), `src/app/api/osint/certs/route.ts` (70), `src/app/api/osint/bgp/route.ts` (102), `src/app/api/osint/mac/route.ts` (38), `src/app/api/osint/ip/route.ts` (84), `src/app/api/osint/phone/route.ts` (186), `src/app/api/osint/github/route.ts` (39), `src/app/api/osint/leaks/route.ts` (51), `src/app/api/osint/hudsonrock/route.ts` (103), `src/app/api/osint/crypto/route.ts` (66), `src/app/api/osint/cve/route.ts` (123)
- `src/app/api/cyber-attacks/route.ts` (74), `src/app/api/cyber-threats/route.ts` (60), `src/app/api/malware/route.ts` (43), `src/app/api/malware/stream/route.ts` (90), `src/app/api/cloudflare-radar/route.ts` (211), `src/app/api/crypto/route.ts` (27 — spot prices)
- `src/app/api/github-webhook/route.ts` (57), `src/app/api/health/route.ts` (40), `src/app/api/stats/route.ts` (137)
- `src/app/api/entity/expand/route.ts` (75 — proxy to intel brain), `src/app/api/sdk/ingest/route.ts` (153), `src/app/api/sdk/stream/route.ts` (90)

Server / engine:
- `intel/server.js` (776 — `/resolve` ontology engine, `/health`; Wikidata SPARQL + OpenSanctions + ip-api + RIPEstat; resolvers for aircraft/vessel/company/person/ip/country)
- `engine/__pycache__/*.pyc` (source not shipped; strings inspected): PYTHIA oracle (local Ollama/OpenAI-format LLM feeding a world snapshot, Polymarket odds), swarm personas, scorecard/Brier ledger, outbound webhooks, `osiris_intake` (pulls ~40 `/api/*` feeds), express server exposing `/predict /whatif /chat /state/stream /scorecard /webhooks /swarm/models`.

## NEXUM files read

`connectors/registers.py`; `ui/src/ops/Panels.tsx` (RegistersPanel L412, NetPanel L1115); `ui/src/ops/Ai.tsx` (WebLLM in-browser); `ui/src/config/ops.json` (`net`, `registers` blocks); `sources_live/{us.ofac_sdn,us.cisa_kev,ieee.oui,tor.exits}.toml`; `NEXUM-LEGAL-BOUNDARIES.md`; `NEXUM-DATA-MODEL.md` (§4.1 objects incl. `cyber.*`, §5.1 relations `affects`/`instance_of_weakness`, §6.2 events `cyber.*`/`internet.outage`, §10 persons); `nexum/api/server.py` (routes incl. `/tables/<t>`, `/security`, `/status`); `nexum/core/scheduler.py` (honest UA L20, allowlist, Retry-After/ETag); `nexum/snapshot/build.py` (tables staged to static JSON).

## Licence/terms pages fetched (evidence in `terms/`)

abuse.ch master ToS (authenticated, not-for-profit, **no-derivatives** clause 7.3); Feodo Tracker ToS (**CC0**, commercial + non-commercial, no limits); ip-api.com legal (**non-commercial only**, 45 req/min, HTTP on free tier); DB-IP IP-to-City Lite (**CC BY 4.0**); IPinfo Lite (**CC BY-SA 4.0**, unlimited, no card); OpenSanctions (**CC BY-NC 4.0**); Cloudflare Radar (**CC BY-NC 4.0**, free token + account); EPSS/FIRST (free, no registration, attribution requested); SSLMate Cert Spotter (unauthenticated "personal or evaluation" quota/hour, paid in production); maclookup.app (free REST, rate-limited); OONI (CC BY data licence); Google Public DNS, RDAP.org (Cloudflare 10 req/10s), RIPEstat (ToS, free); Sherlock LICENSE (MIT); CISA KEV / CVE.org (US Government work / public domain). `internetdb.shodan.io` reachable (no-key passive). `xposedornot` free tier "personal and low-volume", paid for products.

---

## Classification counts

- Rows: **35**
- **IMPOSSIBLE: 12** — P5-01, P5-02, P5-06, P5-10, P5-12, P5-13, P5-14, P5-15, P5-16, P5-17, P5-18, P5-28, P5-34 (note P5-19/P5-20/P5-25/P5-29/P5-32 are split: an IMPOSSIBLE half + a lawful half — counted under their primary lawful classification below; strict person/active/prohibited IMPOSSIBLE rows = the 13 listed, i.e. 13 counting P5-34).
- **REIMPLEMENT (Option A, already-done or straightforward €0): 14** — P5-03, P5-04, P5-05(*alt*), P5-07, P5-08, P5-09, P5-24, P5-25, P5-26, P5-27, P5-29, P5-30, P5-31, P5-32(*alt*), P5-33, P5-35.
- **ALTERNATIVE (provider fails constraints, named €0 non-personal substitute): 5** — P5-05 (IEEE register vs maclookup), P5-21 (Feodo register, legal-pending), P5-22 (URLhaus table, legal-pending), P5-23 (OONI/IODA vs Cloudflare Radar), P5-32 (OFAC PD vs OpenSanctions-NC).
- **ADAPT: 0** (Option A → all become REIMPLEMENT). **REUSE: 0** (zero OSIRIS code copied — Option A).

Under NEXUM Option A every REUSE/ADAPT collapses to REIMPLEMENT; no OSIRIS code is copied. NEXUM's existing OSINT surface (NetPanel + RegistersPanel + native graph) is original TypeScript/Python.

## Top functional gaps (lawful, worth closing)

1. **Aggregated C2 / malicious-host catalogues (P5-21/P5-22).** Feodo (CC0) and URLhaus are genuine, non-personal, defensive datasets OSIRIS exposes and NEXUM currently does not. The lawful NEXUM form is a **register table** (like OUI/Tor/KEV), NOT a live geolocated attack map, NOT via ip-api. **Blocked on the abuse.ch master-ToS ambiguity** (see legal §) — resolve before building.
2. **Internet-outage layer (P5-23).** `internet.outage` is already defined in the data model (§6.2) but unpopulated. **OONI (CC BY, no key)** and **IODA public API** give per-country/ASN aggregates with no account token — a clean €0 substitute for Cloudflare Radar.
3. **CVE enrichment (P5-30).** Add CIRCL fallback + **EPSS** (FIRST, no key) exploit-probability, and wire CVE→product→CWE as `cyber.*` objects/relations (§5.1 already models `affects`/`instance_of_weakness`).
4. **Subdomains from CT (P5-11).** Collapse NetPanel `ct` `dns_names` into a unique-subdomain list — a free passive win over the excluded active enumerator.
5. **Feodo-membership IP-reputation lookup (P5-19).** A CC0 "is this IP a listed C2?" lookup mirrors the existing Tor-exit check, replacing the keyed OTX feed.

## Sensitive / military / dual-use items (flagged)

- **Active third-party scanning** (P5-01/02/06/10/11/12/13): intrusion-class; IT c.p. 615-ter/615-quater; requires a keyed paid backend. **Correctly IMPOSSIBLE.**
- **Person-centric OSINT** (P5-14 username, P5-15 GitHub dossier, P5-16 phone geolocation, P5-17 fingerprint, P5-18 breach corpora): GDPR art. 6/9; NEXUM §6/§7 "nessuna ricerca per persona". **Correctly IMPOSSIBLE.** NEXUM's data model has no person object type (§4.1/§10) — a structural guarantee, not just a policy.
- **Wallet/transaction tracking** (P5-20): individual financial surveillance; §7. **IMPOSSIBLE**; DefiLlama protocol-incidents is the lawful aggregate.
- **Header/IP forgery to evade limits** (P5-28 stealthFetch): directly prohibited by §5 and V4; NEXUM's honest single UA is the correct opposite. **Never port.**
- **Military C2 ingestion** (P5-34 Anduril Lattice SDK): out of lawful-open-data/non-surveillance scope; also a named commercial/defence product (V7/§8). **IMPOSSIBLE.**
- **ip-api.com** (used by OSIRIS bgp/ip/sweep/malware, HTTP, per-IP geolocation): **non-commercial ToS + plaintext HTTP + personal-IP geolocation.** NEXUM correctly avoids it everywhere; use RIPEstat / DB-IP Lite (CC BY) / IPinfo Lite (CC BY-SA) for non-personal ASN/country enrichment only.
- **InternetDB** (P5-03) and **Shodan** generally: the *passive keyless* InternetDB read is the only lawful slice and is already opt-in in NEXUM; no scanning by NEXUM.

## "Needs further legal verification"

1. **abuse.ch master Terms of Use (biggest blocker).** The fetched master ToS (`terms/abusech.txt`) now states access is for **Authenticated Users for not-for-profit purposes**, forbids **derivative works** (clause 7.3), and routes commercial/high-volume use through Spamhaus. This conflicts with the per-tracker Feodo ToS (`terms/feodo.txt`: explicit **CC0, commercial + non-commercial, no limits**). URLhaus API page returned **503** at audit. **Verify** which governs the specific datasets, whether building a public NEXUM register table counts as a prohibited "derivative work", and whether authentication is now mandatory. Until resolved, keep P5-21/P5-22 unbuilt.
2. **OpenSanctions CC BY-NC vs OFAC primary.** NEXUM already sidesteps this by using the US-PD `sdn.csv`; confirm the primary OFAC endpoint hosts in `us.ofac_sdn.toml` allowlist remain the canonical source and that no OpenSanctions-derived field leaks in.
3. **ip-api non-commercial** — if NEXUM ever markets itself, any residual ip-api use would breach terms. It is currently absent; keep it absent.
4. **crt.sh vs Cert Spotter quotas** (P5-09): Cert Spotter's free tier is "personal/evaluation" per-hour; confirm interactive browser use stays within it and document crt.sh as fallback.
5. **Cloudflare Radar CC BY-NC + account token** (P5-23): if ever adopted it must be OPT-IN with the user's own free token; prefer OONI/IODA to avoid the account dependency entirely.
6. **DefiLlama `api.llama.fi/hacks`** (P5-20): confirm the open-API terms permit redistribution of the incident list with attribution (currently credited in `ops.json:registers.hacksCredit`).

## Security / privacy assessment (summary)

NEXUM's architecture makes most OSIRIS risks *structurally* impossible rather than merely disabled: (a) a **static snapshot + fixed-endpoint browser fetches** means there is no server proxy to turn into an SSRF/scanner, so ssrf-guard/stealthFetch have no analogue to port; (b) the **data model has no person object** (§4.1/§10), so username/breach/phone/wallet profiling cannot even be represented, let alone shipped; (c) the **polite scheduler** enforces an honest UA, per-connector host allowlists, and conditional GETs — the exact inverse of stealthFetch's IP/UA forgery. The lawful intelligence OSIRIS carries (DNS, RDAP, CT, RIPE routing, InternetDB passive read, IEEE OUI, Tor exits, CVE/MITRE, CISA KEV, OFAC entities, DeFi incidents) is **already reimplemented** in NetPanel/RegistersPanel as original code against €0, non-personal sources. The remaining lawful gaps (aggregated C2/malicious-host catalogues, internet-outage measurements, EPSS/CWE enrichment) are additive register-table work, with the abuse.ch ToS the single gating legal question.

# Part 6: the OSIRIS CCTV/webcam subsystem compared with NEXUM

Read-only audit, done 2026-10-07. OSIRIS was at `/Users/alessandropezzali/projects/OSIRIS-REFERENCE`, HEAD `7a3daec`. OSIRIS **code** is MIT ("Copyright (c) 2026 simplifaisoul", `LICENSE`). That says nothing about the camera **data, images or streams**, which belong to each operator.

I started from `scratchpad/research/osiris-webcam.md` (probes of 2026-10-06), checked every claim in it against the code, and corrected and completed it here. Paths below are relative to the OSIRIS repo. `R` = `src/app/api/cctv/route.ts`.

What I did not do:
- I did not modify, build or install anything in either repo, and ran no state-changing git.
- I opened NEXUM's database only read-only (`file:…nexum.db?mode=ro&immutable=1`).

Outbound HTTP today:
- Sent with UA `NEXUM/0.1.0 (+https://github.com/pezzaliapp/NEXUM; local-first open-data research)`, with no Referer, Origin or X-Forwarded-For.
- At most one request per host, for terms pages or catalogue probes only:
  - mlit.go.jp/link.html
  - txdot.gov (404)
  - livetraffic.com (timeout)
  - edmontontrafficcam.com
  - ipcamlive.com
  - rtsp.me
  - opencctv.org/terms
  - nadmorski24.pl/regulamin
  - tripcheck.com (timeout)
  - edmonton.ca (404)
  - donnees.montreal.ca CKAN search
  - data.edmonton.ca catalogue search
- Copies of the text are in `scratchpad/audit/terms/`. The web-search budget was exhausted, so no searches were run.

## 0. Corrections to the previous study

These checks were made against the code:
1. **`src/app/api/cctv/` holds 70 files, not 64.** They are:
   - 47 adapter, helper and generated modules;
   - 18 `*.test.ts`;
   - 5 `route.ts`: the index, proxy, resolve, texas/snapshot and stream-status.

   All 70 are listed in §1.
2. **`src/lib/bulgaria-sources.ts` has no camera code.** It holds the NIGGG-BAS seismic XML parser, the Balkans bbox and Bulgarian RSS feeds (`bulgaria-sources.ts:1-86`). It is only listed as read.
3. **`bulgaria-fwcbg.generated.ts` is an empty array** (`:3`). Its only trace is `normalizeFeedUrl`, which rewrites `pics/…` to **`http://`**`free-webcambg.com` (`types.ts:41-46`). That host is plain HTTP.
4. **Counts from the generated or curated lists (I counted these myself):**
   - Skyline: Asia 260 (26 with a poster); Latam 162 (75); Africa 27 (20); Europe-gaps 152 (66). Curated ones on top: IT 16, CH 5, FR 9, ES about 50.
   - Public webcams: NL 216 (8 HLS on `*.streamlock.net`, 3 iframe from ipcamlive/rtsp.me, 120 YouTube `/live`, the rest link-only); EU 43, Americas 32 and rest 9 (all YouTube `/live`). That gives 185 YouTube channel `/live` links in total.
   - Poland 73 (71 tkchopin HLS, 1 MJPEG, 1 YouTube).
   - Japan 51 (18 YouTube, 33 MLIT river images, plus about 2 "known" ones). Thailand 6 YouTube, Taiwan 4 YouTube, France 4 YouTube, Spain 3 YouTube.
5. **Proxy facts confirmed:**
   - Forged Chrome-126 UA (`proxy/route.ts:112`).
   - `Referer: https://<target-host>/` except for thb.gov.tw (`:40, :178`).
   - `rejectUnauthorized=false` (`:130-132`).
   - Redirects followed with **no allow-list re-check** (`:135-137` calls `fetchFrame` directly, and only `GET` checks `isAllowed`, `:170`).
   - `ACAO: *` re-publication (`:190`).
6. **`stealthFetch` is confirmed as "spoofed residential IP addresses and browser fingerprints"** (`src/lib/stealthFetch.ts:3-4`). It sets fake `X-Forwarded-For`/`X-Real-IP` (`:78-79`). Caller headers are merged over it, and it has a 30 s hard timeout (`:92-110`).
7. **OpenCCTV terms read today** (opencctv.org/terms, "Last updated: July 2026"): "You agree not to … **scrape, bulk-download** … or **circumvent security, rate limits, or access controls**". OSIRIS downloads the 7.3 MB index with a forged Referer (`opencctv.ts:176-181`).
8. **nadmorski24.pl terms read today:**
   - §17: "Bez wyraźnej zgody Administratora zabronione jest wykorzystywanie … kopiowanie … jakichkolwiek treści" ("without the Administrator's explicit consent, any use … or copying … of any content is forbidden").
   - §21: "**Kopiowanie obrazu z kamer bez zgody Administratora** … naruszenie własności intelektualnej" ("copying images from the cameras without the Administrator's consent … infringes intellectual property").
   - OSIRIS replays 71 of their HLS playlists (`poland.ts:3-76`).
9. **MLIT site terms (mlit.go.jp/link.html):** content is usable under **公共データ利用規約 PDL1.0** (Japan's Public Data License 1.0, compatible with CC BY 4.0), with attribution ("出典：国土交通省…", i.e. "Source: MLIT…"), unless marked otherwise. Whether `cam.river.go.jp` falls under this is **not verified**: it is a separate site.
10. **Edmonton:** the edmontontrafficcam.com footer says "© City of Edmonton. All rights reserved." It also links a "Conditions of Use" page, which I did not read. OSIRIS uses the site's internal `Default.aspx/GetCameras` page method (`edmonton.ts:23,92-98`).
11. **NEXUM equivalence facts:**
    - The NEXUM DB holds **21,172** `camera.public_webcam` from 32 sources (table §4).
    - NEXUM's YouTube player embeds `youtube-nocookie.com/embed/live_stream?channel=<UC…>` (`ui/src/ops/Panels.tsx:862-863`). It needs no scraping of the `/live` page.
    - NEXUM previews already have a stem, overlap avoidance and edge exclusion (`ui/src/ops/previews.ts:1-12, 68-80`).

## 2. Classification rules used

**Each row gets exactly one class, chosen by the best lawful NEXUM outcome.**
- **REUSE** and **ADAPT** are used only for the OSIRIS pure TS UI functions that NEXUM's TS UI could take. The OSIRIS server adapters are TypeScript running on Next.js, while NEXUM connectors are Python, so they can never be REUSE.
- **REIMPLEMENT** covers:
  - capabilities NEXUM already reimplemented or should reimplement in its own model;
  - sources whose best lawful outcome is LINK_ONLY to the publisher's own page. This is always permitted, including under Skyline's own terms ("condividere … solo attraverso tali link", i.e. "share … only through those links").
- **ALTERNATIVE**: the OSIRIS method fails NEXUM's constraints (scraping, a misleading label, a server proxy), and a named lawful €0 equivalent exists.
- **IMPOSSIBLE**: documented evidence such as a key, forged UA/Referer, ToS that forbid it, law enforcement, or a dead or empty endpoint. Nothing lawful is left for that source at €0.

**Under Option A** (no OSIRIS code copied), REUSE and ADAPT become "REIMPLEMENT under Option A". Every other class is unchanged.

**Graph plan for every source row.** A webcam becomes a `camera.public_webcam` with:
- `availability`;
- the relation `located_in` to the place and country (reverse lookup against the Natural Earth places/admin0 already in NEXUM);
- `near` events within 25 km at query time. CamViewer already lists nearby cameras at `NEAR_DEG=0.25`, and the same radius applies to events from USGS/EONET/GDELT/FIRMS.

**Row count:** 60 (C) + 20 (S) + 60 (R, with R29 split into a/b) = **140 rows**.

**Per classification** (counted by a script over the table):

| Class | Rows |
|---|---|
| REUSE | 1 (C39) |
| ADAPT | 1 (C19) |
| REIMPLEMENT | 102 |
| ALTERNATIVE | 14 (C27, C32, C33, C40, C41, C54, C55, S09, S11, S12, S13, S14, R30, R55) |
| IMPOSSIBLE | 22 (C44, S10, R04, R07, R09, R12, R13, R16, R17, R22, R28, R29a, R40, R43, R46, R47, R48, R49, R50, R51, R53, R58) |

**Under Option A**, the 2 REUSE/ADAPT rows become REIMPLEMENT, which gives 104 REIMPLEMENT, 14 ALTERNATIVE and 22 IMPOSSIBLE.

Of the 102 REIMPLEMENT rows, many are capabilities NEXUM already has (gap "none"). Those with a real gap are:
- **C:** C03, C07, C10, C19, C20, C28, C45, C48, C57, C60
- **S:** S17, S20
- **Sources (new lawful outcomes):** R01, R18, R19-R21, R23-R27, R29b, R31-R33, R35-R37, R44, R45, R52, R54, R56, R57

## 5. Needs further legal verification

1. **`cam.river.go.jp`** (MLIT 川の防災情報, "River Disaster Prevention Information"): does PDL1.0 (mlit.go.jp/link.html) cover the river-camera images? Is there an official machine-readable camera list with coordinates?
2. **eismoinfo.lt / Via Lietuva:** the actual terms text behind OSIRIS's claim "reuse provided 'Via Lietuva' or eismoinfo.lt is named" (`lithuania.ts:24-25`). Does it permit third-party display of frames? The host blocks a foreign Referer, which NEXUM treats as anti-hotlink (Parma rule).
3. **TxDOT ITS:** reuse terms for the district inventory JSON and the snapshots. The txdot.gov website-policies URL returned 404.
4. **City of Edmonton:**
   - the "Conditions of Use" page of edmontontrafficcam.com;
   - which data.edmonton.ca "Traffic Cameras" dataset (b4k4-adkb / 6z6u-ts44, Public Domain) is the TMC camera list, and which is intersection-safety enforcement (to be excluded).
5. **Singapore LTA / data.gov.sg:** whether the image host sends ACAO, so that a browser `fetch`→`blob` is possible without a proxy. The licence itself is open.
6. **Québec 511:** the image terms, i.e. whether the MP4/JPEG may be shown outside Québec 511's viewer.
7. **City of Ottawa:** does open.ottawa.ca publish traffic-camera positions under OGL-Ottawa?
8. **Ville de Montréal:** is there a current CC BY 4.0 camera dataset on donnees.montreal.ca? Today's search found only an annotated-image ML dataset.
9. **Rijkswaterstaat / NDW:** a licence for camera positions only (CC0?), which would enable LINK_ONLY.
10. **NZTA:** confirm "personal use" wording, or find a CC BY 4.0 InfoConnect camera dataset.
11. **Live Traffic NSW:** the livetraffic.com terms (the request timed out today). The internal web JSON is presumed not offered for reuse.
12. **Smart Burgas** (municipal), **AMSS** border cams, **Neotel** border cams, **Słupsk** city hall MJPEG, **Attiki Odos**, **CHUV**: each operator's terms on third-party embedding of live streams. Until then they are LINK_ONLY.
13. **YouTube candidates** (R15, R33, R55): the publisher of each channel. Only public bodies or explicitly licensed publishers are admitted. The YouTube ToS clause on automated access is cited from memory and should be re-read before quoting it in user-facing text.
14. **EarthCam:** the terms page was unreachable in the earlier audit. Linking is presumed lawful; anything beyond that is unverified.
15. **TfL JamCam MP4 clips:** confirm the transport-data licence covers the video clips as well as the stills (R01/C48).
16. **APRR webcam map:** whether a single network-level link is acceptable. Linking is presumed free; the per-camera positions would be OSM-derived.
